# Appendix A: User journey findings

Every finding from the research and check agents, word for word, with its id. The report sections cite these ids in square brackets. "Check" gives the independent checker's verdict: confirmed, corrected (the claim shows the corrected text), unverifiable, unchecked, or added-by-checker. Refuted claims are listed and not used.

218 findings; 184 confirmed or corrected by the checker.

## Live (real-time) online play journey in chess [`ux-live`]

I checked 9 chess products (11 surfaces): Chess.com web and app, Lichess web and mobile, ChessKid, PyChess, Duolingo Chess, Chess Clash, Chess Royale, and the closed chess24 and Play Magnus. I read help centers, store pages and the Lichess, Lichess-mobile and PyChess source code. I also read the OpenAI Apps display-mode rules. The live journey is almost the same everywhere. "Play" is the first menu item. It holds a one-tap quick play with preset time controls, a custom game, play a friend, and play the computer. The waiting state is small and has a Cancel button. Before the main clocks start, a first-move timer runs and either player can abort. In the game, the controls are resign, offer a draw and abort, and some products add takebacks. Chat can be turned off. If a player disconnects, a timer scales with the time control, and then the system ends the game or the opponent claims the win. The end screen shows the result, the rating change, Rematch, New opponent and Analysis or Review. Platforms enforce sportsmanship with warnings and separate pools. Three web platforms let guests play casual live games, but async play and rated play need an account. Platforms with few players (variants, PyChess) use open-seek lobbies and multi-option auto pairing, not quick-pairing pools. Mobile apps keep the screen awake, block back navigation during a game, and use the native share sheet and QR codes. Lichess allows slow third-party clients only for rapid, classical and correspondence games. This is a strong model for the ChatGPT surface, where OpenAI recommends picture-in-picture for games. Limit: the shared web-search budget ran out part way through. Some Chess.com end-screen details are not confirmed, so I marked them with low confidence.

| Id | Check | Claim | Detail | Sources |

| ux-live:F1 | corrected; high; common | Lichess and PyChess let anonymous visitors play casual real-time games; their games are never rated, and correspondence needs an account. Chess.com Guest Play (help 2026-02-10) matches a signed-out player with 'someone who also chose that skill level'; the games are anonymous, and past games and saved analysis need an account. The article does not state that guest games are unrated or that guests meet only guests. | Lichess web: when an anonymous visitor clicks a quick-pairing tile, the client sends an open seek with random color and no rated flag, not a pool entry (ui/lobby ctrl.ts clickPool -> xhr.anonPoolSeek). Correspondence seeks ask an anonymous user to sign up (prompt 'You need an account to do that'). Lichess mobile makes quick-pairing seeks rated only when a user is signed in (GameSeek.fastPairing). Chess.com Guest Play (help page dated 2026-02-10): when signed out, the path is Play Online > pick a skill level > 'Play as Guest' > time control. The guest plays only other guests who picked the same level. Games are anonymous, and there is no history or saved analysis. PyChess: anonymous users ('Anon-' names) can make real-time seeks but not correspondence or two-board seeks. A game with an anonymous player is never rated, and Auto pairing needs sign-in (server/seek.py is_anon_restricted_seek, server/wsl.py). ChessKid and Duolingo need an account. The Chess Clash and Chess Royale store pages do not mention guest play. Count: 3 of the 7 live products allow guest live play (Lichess, Chess.com, PyChess). All 3 make guest games casual only. Examples: Lichess anonymous quick pairing -> casual seek; Chess.com 'Play as Guest' by skill level; PyChess anon: real-time only, never rated. Original claim: Web platforms let a guest play a casual live game before sign-up, but rated play, async play and history need an account. Check: Lichess parts are true. In ctrl.ts, clickPool calls xhr.anonPoolSeek when no user is signed in. xhr.ts posts a hook with color 'random' and no rated field. correspondence.ts shows the 'You need an account to do that' prompt. Lichess mobile GameSeek.fastPairing sets rated: authUser != null. PyChess: seek.py is_anon_restricted_seek blocks anon correspondence (day > 0) and two-board seeks. utils.py rates a game only when both players are not anon. wsl.py skips auto pairing for anon users. Chess.com Guest Play help (2026-02-10) gives the path signed out > Play Online > skill level > 'Play as Guest' > time setting. It says the games are anonymous, and that an account gives past games and saved analysis. But the article does not say 'only other guests'. It says 'you'll be matched with someone who also chose that skill level'. It also does not say whether guest games are rated. So 'all 3 make guest games casual only' is proven for Lichess and PyChess only. | <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/ui/lobby/src/ctrl.ts> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/ui/lobby/src/xhr.ts> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/ui/lobby/src/view/correspondence.ts> <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile/blob/main/lib/src/model/lobby/game_seek.dart> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8615312-what-is-guest-play-i-can-play-without-an-account> <https://github.com/gbtami/pychess-variants/blob/master/server/seek.py> <https://github.com/gbtami/pychess-variants/blob/master/server/wsl.py> <https://github.com/gbtami/pychess-variants/blob/master/server/utils.py> |

| ux-live:F4 | corrected; high; common | Lichess caps a pairing score of about 100-200 points (rating/15 above 1500). Each missed wave subtracts 12 points from the rating gap, up to 460, and other bonuses subtract more. So a player who waits long can be paired across a rating gap of about 560 points or more. The cap tightens the first waves only. | Chess.com (help 2025-04-04): the window starts at ±25, then ±50, and then widens more. By default the cap is ±200 for players below 2500 in bullet or blitz, or below 2000 in rapid. The longest waiter gets priority when candidates are equal. Accounts that often abort, stall or rage-quit can go to a separate pool. Colors come after the match, and the system avoids long runs of one color. Color preference counts only in unrated games. Restarting a seek does not make matching faster. Lichess pools (MatchMaking.scala, PoolList.scala): pairing runs in waves, about every 12-60 s or earlier when 20-40 players wait. It uses weighted maximum matching on rating difference. The largest allowed difference is about 100-200 points, depending on rating. Each missed wave lowers the score by 12 points, up to 460. There are bonuses when both players are provisional or both set compatible ranges. Block lists are respected. Players with similar 'rage-sit' histories are paired together. Marked cheaters and boosters are paired among themselves. Examples: Chess.com ±25 -> ±50 -> ... cap ±200; Lichess waves with +12 points per missed wave. Original claim: Matchmaking pairs only the same time control. It starts with a narrow rating window and widens it as the wait gets longer, with a cap. Check: The Chess.com part is true (help 2025-04-04). It covers ±25, then ±50 'and so on', the default ±200 cap below 2500 in bullet or blitz and below 2000 in rapid, the longest waiter first, a separate pool for abort, stall, rage-quit and sandbag patterns, colors after the match, color preference only in unrated games, and that restarting a seek lowers your chances. The Lichess part is true for the waves: Wave(every 12-60 s, or when 20-40 players wait) in PoolList.scala and PoolActor.scala. It is also true for the miss bonus (misses x 12, at most 460), the provisional, range and rage-sit bonuses, block lists, and cheaters and boosters paired among themselves. One statement misleads: 'the largest allowed difference is about 100-200 points'. ratingToMaxScore (130 below 1000, 100 for 1000-1499, rating/15 from 1500) caps the score, and the score is ratingDiff minus the bonuses. So after many missed waves, the allowed rating gap grows by up to 460 more points, and with a range bonus by 200 more. | <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8639319-how-does-matchmaking-work-in-live-chess> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/pool/src/main/MatchMaking.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/pool/src/main/PoolList.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/pool/src/main/PoolActor.scala> |

It also has invite by username with recent friends, a QR code, and a 'reusable challenge URL'. Open challenges expire after 1 day and other challenges after 2 weeks (Challenge.scala). A decline can carry a reason: later, too fast, too slow, rated, casual, variant and others. Lichess mobile shows a QR code and a 'Share challenge URL' button that opens the native share sheet. Chess.com (help 2026-02-10): Play a Friend from the friend list or search, Challenge from a profile, or the URL chess.com/play/<username>. A live challenge needs the friend online and expires after a few minutes. A daily challenge waits. The Chess.com app can also share a challenge link (help 2024-10-13). PyChess: Copy URL, and 'Host a game for others', where 'the first two people to come to this URL will play with each other'. ChessKid: friends only, and both must be on the Fast Chess page. Chess Clash: invite friends. Count: 7 of 7 live products. Examples: Lichess copy URL + QR + reusable link; chess.com/play/<username>; PyChess host link for two others. Original claim: To play a friend, you share a link (with copy, QR and native share), challenge a username, or use a reusable personal link. The first person who opens the link gets the seat. Check: These parts are true: the Lichess ChallengeUi.scala strings (give this URL, 'The first person to come on this URL will play with you', invite a Lichess user, QR code, reusable challenge URL), the DeclineReason list, the Lichess mobile QR code and 'Share challenge URL' native sheet, Chess.com Play a Friend (2026-02-10: friend list, profile, chess.com/play/<username>, a live challenge 'will expire after a few minutes' if the friend is not online, Daily waits), the Chess.com app 'share the challenge link' (2024-10-13), and PyChess 'The first two people to come to this URL will play with each other.' The expiry statement is incomplete. Challenge.scala sets expiresAt to 1 day for open challenges and two weeks otherwise. But ChallengeApi.sweep marks a real-time (clock) challenge 'offline' when its seenAt is older than 20 s. seenAt is None for open challenges, so they skip this sweep. So a live challenge to a named user dies about 20 s after the challenger's page stops pinging. Only correspondence challenges last 2 weeks. | <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/challenge/src/main/ui/ChallengeUi.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/challenge/src/main/Challenge.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile/blob/main/lib/src/view/game/game_loading_board.dart> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8588467-how-do-i-play-a-friend> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8598418-how-do-i-play-an-unrated-game> <https://github.com/gbtami/pychess-variants/blob/master/client/invite.ts> <https://support.chesskid.com/en/articles/9067136-how-can-my-kid-play-other-kids> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/challenge/src/main/ChallengeApi.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/challenge/src/main/ChallengeRepo.scala> |

First-move time (lila Game.scala): UltraBullet 15 s, Bullet 20 s, Blitz 25 s, Rapid 30 s, Classical 35 s. In tournaments it is shorter (11-30 s). A game expires only while the clock is not running. Chess.com (help 2026-09-07): bullet 15 s, blitz 20 s, rapid 60 s. A missed timer usually aborts the game, but repeat offenders get a loss. Abort help (2026-04-09): abort only 'before either player makes their first move', then Resign, and misuse can turn it off. Original claim: Random-setup variants get special handling: more first-move time, a repeated start position on rematch, and short variant tips. Lag above both shows on the clock. | Chess.com lag forgiveness (help 2026-07-03): rapid gives back 500 ms per move from a 1000 ms bank, blitz 300/400 ms, bullet 200/100 ms. The bank refills every 2 moves, and lag above that shows on the clock. Lichess LagTracker (scalachess): a per-move quota grows by up to min(1 s, 0.4% of estimated game time + 0.15 s) and pays back the lag that the client reports. Lichess mobile socket (socket.dart): ping every 2.5 s. The socket counts as dead after 9 s with no pong. Auto-reconnect starts at 3.5 s and backs off to 60 s. Unacknowledged messages are resent after 1.5 s. Original claim: The server keeps the clocks and gives back network lag from a refillable bank. The mobile client pings often and resends moves that the server has not acknowledged. Check: The Lichess parts are true. LagTracker.maxQuotaGainFor = min(100 cs, estimateTotalSeconds x 2/5 + 15 cs), and quotaGain is also capped by frameLag + 14 cs CPU lag. socket.dart: _kPingDelay 2500 ms, _kPingMaxLag 9 s, _kAutoReconnectDelay 3500 ms, max 60 s, _kResendAckDelay 1500 ms. The default idle socket pings every 25 s, and only the active route pings every 2.5 s. The Chess.com wording is wrong. The help (2026-07-03) says that the per-move amount is always forgiven and that the bank is extra on top of it. It does not say the per-move amount comes from the bank. Rapid: 'always be forgiven for at least 500ms on every move', plus a 1000 ms bank that refills every 2 moves. | <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8615369-what-is-lag-forgiveness-why-did-the-clocks-suddenly-change> <https://github.com/lichess-org/scalachess/blob/master/core/src/main/scala/LagTracker.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile/blob/main/lib/src/network/socket.dart> |

**Product notes**

- **Chess.com (web)**: Play Online has 'Start Game' with the last-used time control, a time selector with 'More Time Controls', Custom Challenge (opponent, variant, rated, rating range, color only if unrated), Play a Friend, Play Bots, and open challenges in a list or chart. Guest Play pairs guests by the skill level they pick. Matchmaking widens from ±25 to a ±200 cap. First-move timers are 15/20/60 s. The disconnect window is 10% of (base + 40 x increment), from 30 s to 3 min. Lag forgiveness uses a bank. There are no takebacks in live games. Chat can be set to everyone, request, friends or off, and Quickchat has presets. The game-over pop-up has Game Review (free daily limit). Variants use a seek lobby, not quick pairing. Help pages are dated 2024-10 to 2026-09. <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8609779-how-do-i-start-a-game-on-chess-com> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8639319-how-does-matchmaking-work-in-live-chess> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8593801-how-does-game-abandonment-work> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8583983-how-do-i-play-chess-variants>
- **Chess.com (iOS/Android app)**: Home > Play gives an instant start or Custom. Play a Friend uses the friend list or search, and the app can share a challenge link. Abort is in the four-line menu with a confirm checkmark. The app has Confirm Each Move, a haptics toggle and home-screen widgets. Bot modes Challenge, Friendly and Assisted give 3, 2 and 1 crowns. Most variants are on the website only (Chess960 is the exception). <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8598418-how-do-i-play-an-unrated-game> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8609803-how-do-i-turn-on-confirm-move> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/9835535-how-can-i-play-chess960-on-the-chess-com-app> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8648611-how-can-i-reset-my-bot-crowns>
- **Lichess (web)**: I read the open-source lila code (master, 2026-10). Home has Quick pairing (11 pools + Custom), a Lobby of seeks, and Correspondence. The buttons are Create a game, Challenge a friend and Play against the computer. Anonymous players get casual seeks. Pools pair in waves and widen 12 points per missed wave. The waiting state is a spinner in the tile, and Esc cancels. First-move time is 15-35 s (x1.5 for Chess960). Abort works until both sides have moved. Players can claim victory or a draw after a grace time that scales with speed. Takebacks need consent. One premove is allowed. Spectator chat stays hidden from players during the game. The end screen has Rematch (colors alternate, the 960 position repeats once), New opponent and Analysis. Bad behavior brings a play ban. <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila> <https://lichess.org/faq>
- **Lichess (mobile app, Flutter)**: I read the source (main, commit 2026-10-09). The Play bottom sheet has the quick-pairing matrix, then Challenge a friend, Correspondence, Arenas, Play against computer (offline) and Over the board. Waiting shows an overlay on an empty board with the settings, live counts and Cancel. A challenge can be shared by QR code or the native share sheet. During a game the screen stays on and back navigation is blocked, and an iOS Live Activity shows the game. The socket pings every 2.5 s, reconnects with backoff, and resends unacknowledged messages after 1.5 s. <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile>
- **ChessKid**: Kids play live (Fast Chess: 5, 10 or 15 min) and slow games. The 2026 help pages describe play only with friends, and both kids must be on the Fast Chess page. An older guide describes automatic pairing by rating, so this may have changed. Kids cannot chat with each other; a third-party parent guide says the only messages are preset icons. Adults can challenge only their own kids or friends. Bots go from 400 to 2200. <https://support.chesskid.com/en/articles/9067136-how-can-my-kid-play-other-kids> <https://www.chesskid.com/learn/articles/how-to-play-fast-slow-matches-and-tournaments2> <https://www.chesskid.com/article/view/how-to-understand-chesskids-safety-features2>
- **PyChess (pychess.org, variants server)**: This open-source server for Fairy-Stockfish variants is the closest analog to King Down. It copies the Lichess UX: Create a game, Play with a friend, Play with AI, and 'Auto pairing' across many variants and time controls with a rating range. Anonymous users can play real-time games, but those games are never rated and they cannot create correspondence seeks. 'Host a game for others' gives a link where the first two visitors play each other. Each variant has strategy tips. Chess960 and Janggi get a 45 s first move in rated games. A player who leaves loses by abandonment after 30-60 s. The end screen has Rematch, New opponent and Analysis board. <https://github.com/gbtami/pychess-variants> <https://www.pychess.org/faq>
- **Duolingo Chess**: PvP launched on iOS in late 2025 and on Android in February 2026. Players open the Matches tab and tap 'Play a Person' to meet an opponent of similar skill. Duolingo community posts say that 3 games against the coach Oscar open PvP and that leaving the app ends the match. As of 2026-02, friend play, game review and web access were still planned. <https://marketech-apac.com/duolingo-launches-chess-pvp-mode-on-android-letting-learners-play-and-practise-languages/>
- **Chess Clash (Miniclip)**: A mobile-first casual game with arenas that have different prize stakes, prize leaderboards, Classic and Quick modes, chat and gifts, friend invites, daily rewards, collectible sets, seasons with a premium pass, and loot boxes. It added async 'Mail Chess' in v8.4-8.12. The store page does not describe how matchmaking works. <https://apps.apple.com/us/app/chess-clash-online-offline/id1543399979>
- **Chess Royale (SayGames)**: A mobile casual game. It says there is 'always a real opponent of similar skill'. It has 8 modes including blitz and tournaments, alerts when friends are online, chat with emoji, achievements, coins and gems, a VIP subscription, puzzles and post-match analysis. Third-party summaries say users complain about matchmaking and ads. <https://apps.apple.com/app/id1434658250>
- **Play Magnus (legacy) and chess24 (closed)**: Play Magnus is a single-player app against a Magnus engine at 30 age levels, with no documented PvP. chess24 had online play and closed on 2024-01-31 after Chess.com bought it. Both now point users to Chess.com. <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Play_Magnus_(mobile_app)> <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chess24>
- **ChatGPT Apps (host for the King Down plugin)**: Display modes are inline, fullscreen and PiP. OpenAI recommends PiP for games that run next to the conversation, and the PiP must react to input from the chat box. On mobile, PiP can show as fullscreen. The widget can call MCP tools. connectDomains covers fetch and XHR, and the docs do not mention WebSocket. <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/concepts/ui-guidelines> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/reference>

**Open questions**

- How many players will be online at the same time at launch? If quick play cannot find a match within about 60 s, should the fallback be the computer, an open-seek lobby only, or async play?
- Can ChatGPT games be rated, given that the assistant in the same chat can analyze the position? The Lichess Board API forbids engine help, but ChatGPT cannot enforce that.
- Does the MCP Apps widget CSP allow WebSocket or Server-Sent Events to a realtime service? The OpenAI reference names only fetch and XHR under connectDomains.
- Haste (two actions in one turn): is the increment added per action or per turn? How do premoves and the first-move timer apply when one side acts twice?
- Card mode with hidden hands: what can spectators see, does spectator chat need a delay, and does a rematch deal new hands?
- Who is the expected audience? If children are likely, chat should default to presets only, as on ChessKid.
- Not confirmed because the shared web-search budget ran out part way through: the exact Chess.com game-over buttons (Rematch or 'New N min'), the design of the Chess.com searching screen, and how Chess Clash and Chess Royale do matchmaking. Someone should check these by looking at the products.
- ChessKid live pairing: the 2026 help pages describe play with friends only, and an older guide describes automatic pairing by rating. Which one is current?
- Duolingo facts (3 games against Oscar to open PvP; leaving the app ends the match) come from community posts by Duolingo staff that I saw only in search snippets. They need a primary source.

## Asynchronous (turn-based, correspondence) play journey [`ux-async`]

I checked 13 products and platforms: Chess.com Daily, Lichess, OGS, Board Game Arena (BGA), Words With Friends 2, Wordfeud, Scrabble GO, Polytopia, Ludo King, Hero Academy, Chess With Friends, Apple Game Center and Play Your Damn Turn. I also checked the ChatGPT plugin runtime. Most facts come from help centers and open source code (lila, the OGS frontend, the Lichess mobile app). The shared web search budget ran out partway through. After that I fetched known help pages directly. Facts for Wordfeud, Polytopia, Ludo King and the current BGA rules are thin. To search more, send a follow-up message.

The most common pattern in hybrid products: asynchronous play is one more time control in the same Play flow, and the home screen shows one game list. Games where it is your turn come first, with a count badge. Chess, Go and board sites let the creator choose days or moves per day. Casual word games use one fixed limit of 5 to 11 days. Servers enforce timeouts with no player online. Chess and Go sites also send a warning when time is low. Notifications check whether you are present: no push while you look at the game, and one email until you come back. Some products also send a daily digest. Random async matches use stored open seeks, not live queues. Some products fill the gap with bots. Reliability numbers (timeout %, average move time) help players pick opponents. Vacation mode is found only on chess and Go sites.

For King Down: put Daily in the Play flow and add a shared "Your turn" inbox. Store deadlines and sweep them on the server. Send presence-aware email and web push from the one account, because I found no proactive notification API for ChatGPT plugins. Use stored seeks with a computer fallback.

| ux-async:F8 | confirmed; high; common | The server ends timed-out games even when no player is online. The default is an automatic loss, but some products let the opponent claim the win. | Lichess 'Titivate' runs every 5 s over games whose checkAt time has passed. It flags out-of-time games 'when no one is around' (QuietFlagCheck). It marks games abandoned after 21 days without a move and deletes unplayed games after 3 days. Each game stores a next-check time: the clock end, daysPerTurn, or 21 days. Chess.com auto-claims timeout wins by default. A player can turn this off and click 'Claim Win' later, but after 60 days the game always ends on time. BGA (deprecated clock doc): a player whose clock goes negative gets a clock penalty, and opponents can skip their turns. The leaver gets a 'leave' penalty and -10 Elo, and the winner's Elo gain is scaled by game progress. Automatic timeout: Lichess, Chess.com, WWF, Scrabble GO and Game Center (5 of 6). A manual claim or skip exists on BGA and as a Chess.com option. Check: Lichess Titivate ('flagged games when no one is around') starts the next pass 5 s after a pass ends. It sends QuietFlagCheck for out-of-time games, sends Abandon after Game.abandonedDays = 21, and deletes games where both players have not moved within 3 days. It sets checkAt from the clock estimate, 3 days for a stopped clock, or daysPerTurn else 21 days. Chess.com (2024-11-13): timeout wins are automatic by default. With the setting off, the player clicks 'Claim Win'. 'If your opponent doesn't make a move within 60 days, they will still lose by timeout.' BGA Game_clock confirms skip, the leave penalty, the extra -10 Elo and the winner's gain scaled by y%. But that page says 'This content is deprecated', so current BGA behavior can be different. | <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/round/src/main/Titivate.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/game/src/main/Game.scala> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8705363-how-can-i-prevent-getting-wins-based-on-timeouts> <https://en.doc.boardgamearena.com/Game_clock> |

**Product notes**

- **Chess.com (Daily chess)**: Daily is a time control in the same Play flow as live chess. The homepage has a 'Daily Games' section (image or list view). Pending challenges show as a board with a plus sign. 'Start Game' looks for a match and makes an open challenge if it finds none. Open challenges show the opponent's average move time and timeout %. Filters: Move Speed, Minimum Games, Avoid Time-Outs (>10%). Vacation is earned (7 days at start; 2 or 3 days a month; cap 15 or 30 days; minimum 24 h), applies to all games, and Premium gets auto-vacation below 90 minutes. Timeout wins are automatic by default, with an optional manual 'Claim Win' and a 60-day hard cap. A game counts after 4 moves. Conditional moves work on web and Android. An 'unavailable' setting blocks new challenges. Emails go out only when the site sees you logged off. Help articles are dated 2024-2026. The after-move option (next game / home / stay) comes from an older article's search snippet. <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8588171-what-is-daily-chess> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8583943-how-does-vacation-work-how-much-time-do-i-get> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8705363-how-can-i-prevent-getting-wins-based-on-timeouts> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8705837-what-is-an-open-challenge>
- **Lichess (correspondence)**: Open source, read from lila master on 2026-10-10. Days per turn: 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10 or 14. Casual Unlimited games exist. The lobby has tabs Quick pairing | Lobby | Correspondence | 'N games in play' with a your-turn badge. Correspondence seeks are stored in the database, with at most 5 per user. A /round-next route opens your next game. Move confirmation is on by default for correspondence. Push goes out only when you are away from the game. A low-time alarm fires at 80% of the remaining time. An opt-in daily email lists your-turn games. A 5-second sweeper flags out-of-time games, abandons games after 21 days and deletes unplayed games after 3 days. Lichess has no vacation. The mobile app (2025-08 release) queues offline correspondence moves. <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila> <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile> <https://lichess.org/@/lichess/blog/mobile-app-official-release/wiwu6goO>
- **OGS (online-go.com, correspondence Go)**: Open source frontend, read from main on 2026-10-10. The nav bar has a turn counter, and one click goes to the next board. Preferences include 'Auto-advance to next game after making a move' and a separate correspondence submit mode. Vacation is manual and pauses all correspondence games. 'Auto-vacation' is on by default. Tournaments can disable vacation. Games can pause on weekends (UTC). There are six clock types and conditional moves. Players can set email notification toggles per type. The accrual numbers (1 day per 8 days, 30-day cap) come from old staff forum posts and may be out of date. <https://github.com/online-go/online-go.com> <https://ogs.readme.io/docs/time-control> <https://forums.online-go.com/t/vacation/12334>
- **Board Game Arena (turn-based vs real-time)**: The same game engine runs both modes, and the table option 'game speed' selects real-time (fast, normal, slow) or turn-based (24, 12, 8, 4, 3, 2 or 1 moves per day, 1 move per 2 days). 'No time limit' is for friends only. Playing hours stop the clock at night (2019). A player who runs out of time can have turns skipped by opponents, gets a leave penalty and -10 Elo. Zombie bots finish multiplayer games. Reputation shows % of games finished and % finished without a clock penalty. The documented notifications are email per turn, one email until you return, and a daily report. The turn-based FAQ wiki is deprecated and undated. I could not read the current FAQ (it needs JavaScript), so current push or app support is unconfirmed. <https://en.doc.boardgamearena.com/Turn_based_FAQ> <https://en.doc.boardgamearena.com/Game_clock> <https://en.doc.boardgamearena.com/Main_game_logic:_Game.php> <https://en.boardgamearena.com/news?id=405> <https://en.doc.boardgamearena.com/Zombie_Mode>
- **Words With Friends 2 (Zynga)**: Async only. Your-turn games move to the 'Your move' tab, with a push notification. 'Pick My Match' is random matching, opt-in through privacy settings. 'Solo Challenge' WordMasters bots can start games with you automatically (opt-out). Auto-resign after 5 days (random opponent) or 11 days (chosen opponent). A green dot shows online players, who you can nudge. Rematch button, chat, block and report. Repeated declines lead to a block prompt. Completed games can be cleared from the list. Wikipedia says up to 40 games at once. Help pages show 'Last Updated: 21h' (about 2026-10-09). <https://zyngasupport.helpshift.com/hc/en/63-words-with-friends-2/> <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Words_with_Friends>
- **Wordfeud**: The homepage (2026) says: up to 30 simultaneous games, random opponents 'at your skill level', friends and chat, 'take hours or minutes per move', and a notification when it is your move. Cross-device play on iOS and Android. I could not find a public FAQ, so the timeout rule is not confirmed. The web search budget ran out before I could check further. <https://wordfeud.com/>
- **Scrabble GO (Scopely)**: Async 1v1 and multiplayer games for up to 4 players, against friends or random players matched by skill. An auto-generated (bot) opponent fills in if no match exists. A game closes after 7 days without a turn, and no loss counts before 4 full turns. Multiplayer games continue after one player resigns. Favourites (past opponents only), chat with a policy, and contact-list friend finding. Help pages are dated about 2023-2026 (relative 'Last Updated' values). <https://scopely.helpshift.com/hc/en/28-scrabble-go/>
- **The Battle of Polytopia**: Online multiplayer since 2018-02-15, cross-platform from the start (iOS and Android). It has ranked games. The Steam store lists cross-platform multiplayer, 'Steam Turn Notifications' and matchmaking for up to 16 players. Tournaments are under Multiplayer > Tournaments (2025-2026 blog). The 2025 balance pass shows a '60 days' turn time limit option. I could not confirm the exact timer choices or the split between live and async play (the fandom wiki blocks fetches). <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Battle_of_Polytopia> <https://store.steampowered.com/app/874390/The_Battle_of_Polytopia/> <https://polytopia.io/2025-balance-pass/>
- **Ludo King**: The FAQ documents invites and challenges to Facebook friends, chat block and mute, player reports, account sync, and web play at ludokinglive.com. It documents no async or turn-timeout features. It seems to be a live-session product. Evidence is thin (FAQ only). <https://ludoking.com/faq/>
- **Hero Academy (Robot Entertainment)**: An early cross-platform async tactics game: two players, five actions per turn, units from a refilled hand (a close match to King Down's card mode). It came to Steam on 2012-08-10. It and Hero Academy 2 shut down on 2019-04-08. Wikipedia does not describe its notification or timeout rules. <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hero_Academy>
- **Chess With Friends (Zynga)**: I found no Chess With Friends help center among Zynga's current help centers, while Words With Friends 2, Boggle and Crosswords with Friends have one. It is probably retired, but I could not confirm this (low confidence). The web search budget ran out. <https://zyngasupport.helpshift.com/hc/en/>
- **Apple Game Center (GameKit turn-based)**: A platform reference for async design. Default turn timeout is 1 week, maximum 90 days. On timeout, Game Center passes the turn to the next participant. saveCurrentTurn and endTurn are separate calls. Each turn has a message that shows in the notification. sendReminder works as a nudge. The matchmaker lists 'Your Turn' and 'Their Turn'. Automatch fills empty seats, and invitations go out after the first turn. Game Center stores matches until the player removes them. <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/gamekit/gkturnbasedmatch> <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/gamekit/starting-turn-based-matches-and-passing-turns-between-players> <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/gamekit/creating-turn-based-games>
- **Play Your Damn Turn (Civilization async service)**: A small community service for async Civilization games. Vacation mode has three host options: skip the turn at timer end (default since 2023-07), skip immediately, or pause the game for the player on vacation. <https://discourse.playyourdamnturn.com/t/fixed-ability-to-disable-turn-timer-for-players-on-vacation-mode/8031>
- **ChatGPT plugins (Apps) runtime**: The widget can call tools (callTool), post a follow-up message, keep widget state, open external links (openExternal) and request fullscreen or picture-in-picture. I found no documented proactive notification to users. Async play in ChatGPT therefore depends on the player coming back, or on email or web push from the website account (inference). The guidelines require that the plugin is not a worse version of the website. <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/reference> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/app-guidelines>
- **Web platform (kingdown.dev)**: Web Push works in desktop browsers, and on iOS or iPadOS 16.4+ only for Home Screen web apps. Permission needs a user action. The Badging API can show a your-turn count on an installed app icon, but support is limited. <https://webkit.org/blog/13878/web-push-for-web-apps-on-ios-and-ipados/> <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Badging_API>

**Open questions**

- Does the ChatGPT plugin platform have any proactive notification, scheduled-task hook or deep link that reopens a specific conversation or board? I found none in the reference (F31). Please confirm with OpenAI.
- Can an email or web-push link open the opponent's game inside ChatGPT, or must links always open kingdown.dev?
- Should rated Daily games share the live rating or have a separate Daily rating? This is an owner decision. Chess.com and Lichess keep separate ratings per speed (not checked in this research).
- In a Haste turn, is the clock per turn (recommended) or per action? When does the opponent get the 'your turn' notification?
- Card mode with hidden hands: which game facts can a notification or the inbox show without leaking hidden information?
- Default Daily time: 1, 3 or 7 days? Allow unrated 'no time limit' games with friends?
- Should vacation be in version 1? If yes, which accrual and cap?
- Which email provider and budget? Web push also needs VAPID keys and a service worker on kingdown.dev.
- Not confirmed because the shared web search budget ran out: Wordfeud's timeout rule; Polytopia's timer choices and live/async split; BGA's current push/app support and turn-based limits; whether Chess With Friends is retired; whether Chess.com has a nudge. Send a follow-up message to continue the search.

## Journeys in other online board, strategy and card games [`ux-other`]

I checked 18 products: Board Game Arena (BGA), OGS, Lishogi, 81Dojo, PyChess, Lichess (web and mobile), Backgammon Galaxy, Hearthstone, Marvel Snap, Legends of Runeterra, Clash Royale, Colonist, Catan Universe, Tabletopia, Chess.com 4 Player Chess, Yomi, Duelyst and 5D Chess. The patterns below are the most common.

1. The home screen gives three ways to start a game: play the computer, play a friend, and find an opponent. A list of your games in progress sits near the top.
2. Every product splits casual games from ranked games. Friend games use a link or a short code and usually do not count for rating.
3. Most sites have both a quick "Play now" button and a list of open challenges.
4. Small communities keep their players in one pool. They remove queue options, merge rating categories, and let one player wait in several queues at the same time.
5. Bots fill gaps. Commercial card games match new or low-rated players against AI opponents. Some games let you play a bot while you wait for a human.
6. Board-game and go sites put asynchronous (turn-based) play at the center. These games need day-based clocks, "your turn" notices, a vacation mode and rules against quitting. Commercial card games are live only.
7. Scheduled events for small variants get few players: often 0 to 6 per event.
8. Games that are only player against player and serve a small audience can fail. Duelyst shut down in 2020. Riot moved Legends of Runeterra to its player-versus-computer mode in 2024.

For King Down: keep computer play and crowns as the core. Make friend play and async play the first multiplayer features. Use one live queue with a clearly labeled bot fallback. Send turn notices through the website and email.

Note: the shared web-search budget ran out partway through. I then read only pages whose addresses I already knew, so some claims about card games have low confidence.

**Product notes**

- **Board Game Arena**: Lobby with Automatic ('Play now', 2017) and Manual tables. You can queue for several games at once. Real-time is the main mode; turn-based runs at 1–24 moves per day with playing hours (2019). Players choose email turn notices or a daily digest. Karma score 0–100 (2016). Arena seasons (3 months, league-based; BGA reported +20% games in 2020). Optional community tutorials for about 130 games. Tournaments: Swiss, elimination, round-robin (community forum). The FAQ page did not render, so I used the news posts and the doc wiki. <https://en.boardgamearena.com/news?id=341> <https://en.doc.boardgamearena.com/Turn_based_FAQ> <https://en.boardgamearena.com/news?id=405> <https://en.boardgamearena.com/news?id=304> <https://en.boardgamearena.com/news?id=430>
- **OGS (online-go.com)**: Play page split into Quick Match and Custom Games tabs (Oct 2024), with options cut to grow the pool. Speeds: blitz, rapid, live, correspondence. Community bots under 'Computer'. Correspondence ladders, tournaments of many types, automatic site-wide tournaments that cancel when not filled, and hourly live tournaments. Cancel window until move 6. Vacation mode. <https://forums.online-go.com/t/upcoming-changes-to-the-play-page-and-general-time-settings/53460> <https://github.com/online-go/online-go.com/wiki/Finding-&-Starting-a-Game> <https://github.com/online-go/online-go.com/wiki/OGS-Ladders> <https://github.com/online-go/online-go.com/wiki/Tournaments>
- **Lishogi**: A Lichess fork. Home has Create a game, Play with a friend and Play with the computer, plus TV, puzzle and leaderboard. 432 players online on 2026-10-10. Dec 2025: five real-time ratings merged into one, lobby presets with a rating range, computer levels on Fairy-Stockfish and YaneuraOu. Glicko-2 starting at 1500±700 with a provisional '?'. Many community round-robin and arena events. <https://lishogi.org/> <https://lishogi.org/blog/post/aUhtgBEAAC0ABLJI> <https://lishogi.org/faq> <https://lishogi.org/tournament>
- **81Dojo**: Non-profit shogi server sponsored by the Japan Shogi Association. About 100k registered users (2020). A classic lobby with a player list, a waiting list and a game list with watcher counts. You can wait for a game and use auto-pairing at the same time; a long wait leads to a confirmation step. Challenges must be accepted within 20 s. Password rooms only for non-rated games. Bots use a 'COM_' prefix and can be hidden. Shared analysis after the game and study rooms. Guests can play; rated games need an account. Manual sources date from 2013 to about 2019. <https://81dojo.com/documents/81Dojo_Manual> <https://sites.google.com/view/dojo-manual/English/EN-Chapter1-Desktop> <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/81Dojo>
- **PyChess (pychess.org)**: Open-source variant server (Python with aiohttp, MongoDB, Fairy-Stockfish, fairyfishnet). Lobby seeks plus auto-pairing across several variants and time controls. Invite-friend seeks. AI from Random-Mover up to Fairy-Stockfish level 8. Correspondence seeks last weeks, with Web Push for 'your turn'. Discord bot posts seeks with a ping for each variant. Community variants are always casual. Monthly and weekly variant arenas draw 0–6 players. OAuth login through Lichess, Lishogi, Google or Discord. <https://github.com/pychess/pychess-variants> <https://github.com/pychess/pychess-variants/blob/master/server/auto_pair.py> <https://github.com/pychess/pychess-variants/blob/master/server/seek.py> <https://github.com/pychess/pychess-variants/blob/master/server/discord_bot.py> <https://github.com/pychess/pychess-variants/blob/master/server/push_notifications.py> <https://www.pychess.org/tournaments>
- **Lichess (web and mobile)**: Web home: Create lobby game, Challenge a friend, Play against computer, plus open tournaments and TV. Quick pairing uses 11 fixed pools paired in waves. Mobile bottom tabs: Home, Puzzles, Learn, Watch, More. The home tab has a floating Play button, a quick-pairing grid and an ongoing-games carousel that includes correspondence games, with offline correspondence play. <https://lichess.org/> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/modules/pool/src/main/PoolList.scala> <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile/blob/main/lib/src/tab_navigation.dart> <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile/blob/main/lib/src/view/home/home_tab_screen.dart>
- **Backgammon Galaxy**: 'Play Now' is the main button, with 'Play a Friend', 'Practice vs AI' (beginner to expert), daily tournaments (rated since Jul 2026), a lobby under 'More', and lessons and analysis. States 100k+ games per day. No async mode seen. The ratings page returned 404. <https://www.backgammongalaxy.com/> <https://www.backgammongalaxy.com/changelog/>
- **Hearthstone**: Skippable tutorial, then the Apprentice Track (25 levels, Dec 2023), which locks most modes. New-player pools in Casual and Arena. AI opponents for Apprentice and low-MMR players on ranked Standard (Sep 2022) with no effect on MMR. Friendly Challenge (unranked) and spectating through the friends list. Modes: Casual, Ranked, Battlegrounds, Arena, Tavern Brawl, solo. Main sources: community wiki and news coverage of the patch notes. <https://hearthstone.wiki.gg/wiki/New_player_experience> <https://hearthstone.wiki.gg/wiki/Apprentice_Track> <https://hearthstone.wiki.gg/wiki/Matchmaking> <https://hearthstone.wiki.gg/wiki/Game_modes> <https://outof.games/news/5421-hearthstones-patch-244-is-now-live-maw-and-disorder-mini-set-mercenaries-and-battlegrounds-updates-low-rating-ai-opponents-more/>
- **Marvel Snap**: Games of about 6 turns with simultaneous reveal. Ranked with monthly soft resets, Friendly Battle by match code (Jan 2023), Conquest leagues (Jun 2023), and limited-time modes. Bots confirmed; the developer says they weigh MMR and collection level (Aug 2025). Fan sites say bots are unlabeled and fill gaps after about 5 s (community). <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvel_Snap> <https://marvelsnapzone.com/developer-update-for-the-week-of-august-31-2025/> <https://marvelsnapzone.com/bots-in-marvel-snap-a-comprehensive-guide/>
- **Legends of Runeterra**: Normal and Ranked PvP, VS AI, Challenges, friend challenges, and Path of Champions (PvE roguelike, Nov 2021) after the tutorial. In Jan 2024 Riot moved its focus from PvP to Path of Champions after layoffs blamed on underperformance. The current Play tab is not verified (the search budget ran out). <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legends_of_Runeterra>
- **Clash Royale**: 3-minute battles. Trophy progression, ranked leagues, 2v2, events, Clan Wars. Training Camp against a skilled bot and practice against a past opponent's deck (Oct 2023). Supercell has not confirmed bots in live matchmaking. Friendly battle details not verified (the fandom wiki was blocked). <https://supercell.com/en/games/clashroyale/blog/release-notes/app-store-update-october-23-2023> <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clash_Royale>
- **Colonist.io**: Home has 'Play with Friends' and 'Play Online'. Navigation: Leaderboards, Rooms, Play, Store. Room invite link, Ranked 1v1 (about 15 min), Ranked Rush (4 players, seasons). Bots fill private-room seats and have difficulty levels (2024). Karma filter (2023), auto-reconnect, replays and Beginner mode (2024). 532K monthly active players and peak concurrency of 7.1K in 2024. Rush (2026) is simultaneous and lasts about 10 minutes. <https://colonist.io/> <https://blog.colonist.io/colonist-io-2024-summary/> <https://blog.colonist.io/colonist-io-2023-summary/> <https://blog.colonist.io/colonist-rush-development/> <https://blog.colonist.io/colonist-rush-faq/>
- **Catan Universe**: Online play with friends, offline play against strong AI, leaderboards and seasons, and 'One account, all devices' across Android, iOS, Mac and Windows. The site notes some features are limited after a backend platform change. I did not find async play. <https://www.catanuniverse.com/en/>
- **Tabletopia**: Sandbox tabletop with no AI. Rooms are shared by link or 6-digit code; guests join free and the group needs one account. 'Visible to other players' publishes the room in Find & Play. Players use Discord and Find & Play chat to find opponents. Scheduled games are possible. Room persistence is 15 min on free plans and 30 days with a premium user. <https://help.tabletopia.com/knowledge-base/how-to-find-opponents/> <https://help.tabletopia.com/knowledge-base/starting-games-and-inviting-players/>
- **Chess.com 4 Player Chess**: Free-for-All and Teams modes on a 160-square board. Teams have private chat and arrows. Often the top variant by concurrent players, with a yearly championship since 2019. I could not render the queue and rating screens. <https://www.chess.com/terms/4-player-chess> <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four-player_chess>
- **Yomi (Sirlin Games)**: While waiting in quick match, a player can choose a bot opponent and play. A human can still be matched in, and the player can then concede the bot game without a rating loss (forum, 2017). <https://forums.sirlingames.com/t/playing-bots-while-waiting-for-a-match/1796>
- **Duelyst**: Grid board (5×9) with cards and generals; ranked ladder, practice, puzzle challenges and a draft mode. Servers shut down Feb 2020 because of declining player counts. Source released as CC0 in 2023. A warning case for a niche PvP game that mixes a board and cards. <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duelyst>
- **5D Chess with Multiverse Time Travel**: Chess variant on Steam (2020) with online play, local play, an offline AI (reviewers call it weak) and puzzles. The article does not describe its matchmaking. <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5D_Chess_with_Multiverse_Time_Travel>

**Open questions**

- Can an OpenAI Apps or MCP app notify a ChatGPT user when it is their turn, or must all turn notices go through kingdown.dev email or Web Push?
- How many players will be online at the same time after launch, by hour of day? This decides whether a live queue is useful at all or should only open during announced 'play hours'.
- Haste can give one side two actions in a turn. Should an async turn notice go out only when control really passes to the other player, and how should the 'your turn' list show this?
- For the hidden-hand card mode, is async play acceptable? This needs the server (not the client) to hold hands, as the Supabase match authority already does for moves.
- Should bot games played while waiting give crowns, and should a human match be allowed to interrupt a bot game in progress?
- Should Saar label bot opponents in every case? Commercial games hide bots; I recommend labels, but this is a product and ethics choice.
- The shared web-search budget ran out during this research. I could not verify: current Clash Royale, Marvel Snap and Hearthstone bottom-navigation layouts; how Clash Royale friendly battles work; Chess.com 4PC queue and rating screens; LoR's current Play tab; and online chess variants with powers or cards other than Duelyst and 5D Chess. A follow-up run can check these.

## Multiplayer games inside a host app or chat surface (analogs for ChatGPT) [`ux-embedded`]

ChatGPT is the weakest social host of the platforms checked. Other in-chat game hosts (iMessage, Discord, Telegram, Messenger, LINE, Slack, Reddit, Teams) give some or all of these: a shared thread, a native invite picker, host-signed identity, and a turn alert through the host. ChatGPT plugins give none of these to consumer users. Each player is in a private conversation. Group chats began to retire on 2026-07-09. MCP Apps has no share API. MCP Events (server-to-ChatGPT webhooks) works only in Work chats and dots. The iframe can close at any time. ChatGPT gives only an anonymized user ID, so a real identity comes from your own OAuth. This fits King Down well: one Supabase account can serve the website and ChatGPT.

What maps to ChatGPT: the compact-versus-expanded pattern (an inline card for the lobby and status, fullscreen for the board), deep-link invites that carry a token, mixed auth without a login wall, a server that keeps the match state, and turn alerts on King Down's own channels (email or web push). Live sync in the board can use a direct WebSocket that the plugin declares in connectDomains. Cloudflare's ChatGPT chess demo does this. Two ChatGPT-specific risks: the model can see the board and help a player in a rated game, and a board that reloads can show old data.

Limits: the shared web-search budget ran out during the research. After that, I used only direct fetches of primary docs. Some product details (the chess apps in the ChatGPT directory, Discord's Chess in the Park, and the ChatGPT Messages tab) are not fully confirmed.

Combined with 'On mobile, picture-in-picture may be presented as fullscreen', this favors a fullscreen board plus a small inline status card, not PiP as the main surface. | <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/reference.md> |
| ux-embedded:V4 | added-by-checker; medium | Event-driven updates in ChatGPT reach only some users: dots (consumer Pro plans and some business plans) and Work chats. The user must subscribe in that chat, so the main 'your turn' channel must live outside ChatGPT. | Dots are rolling out to Pro 100/200/500 (18+, outside the EEA, UK and Switzerland) and to Business Premium and Enterprise (off by default). MCP Events runs a task in the subscribed chat that follows the user's instructions. ChatGPT's notification settings list push, email and SMS channels, but no doc says that plugin events trigger them. Recommendation: put email or web push from kingdown.dev at the center of async play, and treat MCP Events as an optional extra for Pro users. | <https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/dots> <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/build/mcp-events.md> <https://learn.chatgpt.com/codex/notifications> |
| ux-embedded:V5 | added-by-checker; medium | OpenAI's own docs do not promise WebSocket support in a ChatGPT widget, and review compares the declared CSP with what the UI does. | The ChatGPT reference describes connectDomains only as 'Domains the widget may contact via fetch/XHR' and says 'The CSP controls standard fetch requests'. Only the MCP Apps spec mentions WebSocket. The Cloudflare demo that uses a WebSocket declares no CSP at all, and it uses the legacy skybridge MIME type. Test a declared wss origin in ChatGPT web, desktop and mobile before you replace the 3-second polling. Polling through callServerTool, or fetch to a declared https origin, is the documented path. | <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/reference.md> <https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps/blob/main/specification/draft/apps.mdx> <https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/blob/main/openai-sdk/chess-app/src/index.ts> |

**Product notes**

- **ChatGPT plugins (Apps SDK / MCP Apps)**: Hosts an iframe board through MCP Apps: inline card or carousel, fullscreen, and PiP (PiP becomes fullscreen on mobile). Identity: anonymized openai/subject, plus your own OAuth 2.1. Mixed noauth and oauth2 auth works per tool. No shared thread (group chats retiring since 2026-07-09), no share API, no presence, and no consumer push (MCP Events only in Work chats and dots). The iframe can close at any time, and after a refresh toolOutput can be old. A direct WebSocket works if the plugin declares it in connectDomains. Digital goods and ads are not allowed. <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/concepts/ui-guidelines.md> <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/reference.md> <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/build/mcp-events.md> <https://developers.openai.com/plugins/plugin-guidelines.md>
- **Cloudflare multiplayer chess demo for ChatGPT**: Reference build (guide updated 2026-06-03). One render tool, and the widget calls the game Durable Object through a useAgent WebSocket. The game ID goes in widgetState. Join means pasting the game ID. Identity is an anonymous UUID in localStorage, and the MCP endpoint has no auth. 'Ask for help' uses sendFollowUpMessage with the FEN. <https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/guides/chatgpt-app/> <https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/blob/main/openai-sdk/chess-app/src/app.tsx>
- **Chessy (ChatGPT directory)**: Single-player chess against ChatGPT, developer Altmatter LLC. Known only from a search snippet, because the page returns 403. No multiplayer. <https://chatgpt.com/plugins/plugin_asdk_app_69a0e374670c819190761772d2092135>
- **iMessage apps / GamePigeon**: The thread is the lobby, the invite and the alert. An MSSession message replaces the previous bubble and leaves a summary. State goes in a URL of at most 5,000 characters. Identity is a per-device UUID, with no account. A live layout needs a fallback layout, and the URL opens a web page on macOS. GamePigeon: 24 games including Chess, 4.0 from 234K ratings, latest version 2024-09-16, Apple devices only. <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/messages/mssession> <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/messages/msmessage/url> <https://apps.apple.com/us/app/gamepigeon/id1124197642>
- **Discord Activities (Embedded App SDK; Chess In The Park, Putt Party)**: Iframe Activities started in channels, DMs or the App Launcher, GA since 2024-09-26. Each instance ends when the last participant leaves. The SDK has openInviteDialog, shareLink with a custom_id for turn-based deep links, setActivity rich presence, participant events and FOCUSED, PIP and GRID layouts. Identity is Discord OAuth, exchanged on the server. Voice and small groups (3 to 8) raise retention. Chess In The Park: many games at once against anyone on the server. <https://docs.discord.com/developers/developer-tools/embedded-app-sdk> <https://docs.discord.com/developers/activities/design-patterns> <https://docs.discord.com/developers/activities/development-guides/growth-and-referrals> <https://docs.discord.com/developers/change-log> <https://discord.com/api/v10/applications/832012774040141894/rpc>
- **Telegram Games (Bot API gaming platform)**: The game message always has a Play button. setGameScore edits the chat scoreboard in place. A service message goes to the chat on a new high score. shareScore needs a direct user action. <https://core.telegram.org/bots/games> <https://core.telegram.org/bots/api#setgamescore>
- **Telegram Mini Apps**: startapp deep links give start_param. In the main Mini App, chat_instance supports 'multiplayer games' for each chat. initData is signed (HMAC or Ed25519) and checked on the server. shareMessage and prepared messages for invites. requestWriteAccess lets the bot send messages to the user, which can carry turn alerts. activated and deactivated lifecycle events. Fullscreen and home-screen shortcut (Bot API 8.0, 2024-11-17). <https://core.telegram.org/bots/webapps>
- **Facebook / Messenger Instant Games**: The context is the thread. updateAsync posts a card with image, text, CTA and data (at most 1000 characters) to the thread. Strategies: IMMEDIATE, LAST and IMMEDIATE_CLEAR. Alerts: PUSH or NO_PUSH, with rate limits. The recipient re-enters with getEntryPointData. Async matchmaking uses offlineMatch. The Messenger bot has a 24-hour window and the GAME_EVENT tag. The docs are undated, so Messenger's status in 2026 is not confirmed. <https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/games/retain/custom-updates> <https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/games/retain/play-with-friends> <https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/games/retain/notifications/game-updates-messenger>
- **Slack**: No iframe. A game can be a Block Kit message that chat.update edits in place (only the app's own messages, Tier 3). App Home gives each user a persistent personal dashboard, which the app updates with views.publish even when the user is away. That suits a list of the user's own games. <https://docs.slack.dev/reference/methods/chat.update> <https://docs.slack.dev/surfaces/app-home>
- **LINE (LIFF / LINE MINI App)**: shareTargetPicker sends Flex or text messages as the user to chosen friends or groups. The ID token (JWT) goes to the server. Chat-room IDs were removed on 2023-02-06. Service messages go only in response to a user action, at most 5 per action, no ads. <https://developers.line.biz/en/reference/liff/> <https://developers.line.biz/en/docs/line-mini-app/develop/service-messages/>
- **Reddit Devvit (games in posts)**: Inline mode for menus and leaderboards, expanded mode for the full game (user-initiated). Realtime channels with sends from the server only, and Redis for state. Logged-out players can play, the login prompt comes later, and state carries over the login. Push is a gated beta: opt-in, at most 2 per day, short copy, local daytime. <https://github.com/reddit/devvit-docs/blob/main/docs/capabilities/realtime/overview.md> <https://github.com/reddit/devvit-docs/blob/main/docs/guides/logged-out-users.mdx> <https://github.com/reddit/devvit-docs/blob/main/docs/capabilities/notifications/pn-best-practices.md>
- **Microsoft Teams Live Share**: The host gives a free Fluid relay for meetings, chats and channels, with LivePresence, LiveState and LiveTimer. Hosted data lasts at most 24 hours, so durable state needs your own service. Its 'coincidental collaboration' shows who views what. <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoftteams/platform/apps-in-teams-meetings/teams-live-share-overview>
- **MCP Apps standard (SEP-1865)**: The open UI standard behind ChatGPT plugins, also adopted by other MCP hosts. It defines connectDomains (including WebSocket), clipboardWrite permission, visibility ['model','app'], ui/message, ui/open-link, ui/request-display-mode, teardown, and app-exposed tools (its example is tic-tac-toe). The draft was created 2025-11-21, and a dated 2026-01-26 version exists. <https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps/blob/main/specification/draft/apps.mdx>

**Open questions**

- The harness's shared web-search budget ran out mid-research, so the later checks used only direct fetches of primary pages. Should a follow-up search check the full ChatGPT plugin directory for multiplayer or board-game plugins, and Discord's Chess In The Park UX?
- Does ChatGPT throttle or rate-limit app-initiated tools/call? This matters for the current 3-second polling.
- Does ChatGPT accept a wss:// origin in ui.csp.connectDomains in October 2026, and does it keep a WebSocket open when the widget scrolls off screen, or when the ChatGPT mobile app goes to the background?
- Does ChatGPT grant the MCP Apps clipboardWrite permission, so that 'Copy invite link' works in the board?
- Is there a supported ChatGPT deep link that opens a new chat with a plugin and a prefilled prompt? It would make the 'Play in ChatGPT' choice on the join page a single tap.
- What is the new ChatGPT 'Messages' tab (reported July 2026)? Can users send links or plugin content to each other through it? Will MCP Events or a consumer notification API reach personal ChatGPT accounts?
- Do plugin widgets render in shared ChatGPT conversation links, and does an old widget re-mount when the user scrolls back to it?
- Should games played from ChatGPT be rated at all, given that the model is always next to the player? Or should ChatGPT games count only when the position stays hidden from the model?
- Are Instant Games still live in Messenger threads in 2026? The current Meta docs list Messenger but are undated.
- Do the public plugin review rules (demo account, no extra sign-up steps) apply to a private plugin that only the owner and invited players use?

## Best practices and official guidelines for multiplayer game UX [`ux-guidelines`]

Official guidelines and the products I checked agree on one main point: let people play first, and ask for an account, notifications or chat only when they give a clear benefit. The sources are Apple HIG, Google Play Games Services, Xbox XAGs, WCAG 2.2, the Game Accessibility Guidelines, NN/g, Hodent, the OpenAI Apps SDK, Lichess source code, the Chess.com help center, Words With Friends, Hearthstone, Board Game Arena and League of Legends.

Key patterns:
- **Accounts:** Guest play is normal. Chess.com and Lichess both let guests play humans, and rated play needs an account.
- **Notifications:** Ask for permission only after a user action. Give per-event settings. Send few notifications: Lichess sends one reminder per game and one daily digest.
- **Live games:** Every product I checked gives the remaining player a timed path to a result. The disconnect timer scales with the time control and is shorter when the leaver is losing. Repeat leavers get gradual, private penalties.
- **Async games:** Each turn has a deadline (Lichess default 2 days, Game Center 1 week), one reminder and vacation days.
- **Chat:** For strangers, use presets and emotes, with mute, block and report. Lichess also has a kid mode that turns off all communication. The ChatGPT audience includes ages 13 to 17.
- **Accessibility:** Give a click-to-move alternative to dragging. Announce turn and disconnect events through live regions. Offer untimed or async play as the alternative to clocks.
- **ChatGPT:** Per-tool optional sign-in matches lazy registration. Picture-in-picture mode suits live games. The Apps SDK documents no push or background updates, so async turns need email or the website.

Limits: the shared web-search budget ran out partway through. After that I used WebFetch, help centers and GitHub source. I could not verify facts for Clash Royale, Marvel Snap, Roblox, Fortnite or the Board Game Arena FAQ.

The reference page says 'On mobile, picture-in-picture may be presented as fullscreen.' | <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/concepts/ui-guidelines> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/reference> |
| ux-guidelines:F29 | corrected; medium | The Apps SDK documents no push, background or server-initiated widget update, and no way to alert a player inside ChatGPT when the board is closed. While the board is open, data does not come only from tool results: a widget may contact the domains it declares in _meta.ui.csp.connectDomains through fetch or XHR. So an open board can poll the game server directly, without a model-visible tool call for each poll. | The reference lists requestDisplayMode, callTool, sendFollowUpMessage, widgetState and setWidgetState, but no push or background update API. Data reaches the widget only through tool results. This is an absence of evidence, so confidence is medium. The submission guidelines add: a privacy policy and minimum data. Selling digital goods or promoting upgrades is not allowed. Irreversible actions (such as a resign tool) need destructiveHint. Plugins must work on desktop and mobile. Original claim: The Apps SDK documents no push, background or server-initiated widget updates, so an async ChatGPT game cannot alert the player inside ChatGPT when the turn comes. Check: The reference lists window.openai APIs (callTool, sendFollowUpMessage, setWidgetState, requestDisplayMode, requestModal, openExternal, uploadFile) and only host-driven notifications (ui/notifications/tool-input, openai:set_globals), with no server push. But the reference defines connectDomains as 'Domains the widget may contact via fetch/XHR'. The submission guideline parts are confirmed: privacy policy, minimum data, no digital goods or upgrades, destructiveHint true for irreversible effects, works on desktop and mobile. | <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/reference> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/app-submission-guidelines> |
| ux-guidelines:F30 | confirmed; high | Reconnection UX depends on fast detection, automatic retry with backoff, resending unacknowledged moves, and a visible 'Reconnecting' state, while the server stays the authority for clocks. | Lichess mobile socket.dart (last commit 2026-10-09): a ping every 2.5 s, and the connection counts as lost after 9 s of lag. It reconnects after 3.5 s, then backs off exponentially to 60 s after a 60 s grace period. Unacknowledged messages are resent after 1.5 s. The socket disconnects after 1 min in the background. Lichess web shows a 'Reconnecting' indicator. Chess.com: the disconnect timer runs only on the leaver's turn. A disconnected player's board 'may not match' the real game state, so the client must re-sync from the server. Check: lichess mobile socket.dart: ping 2500 ms, max lag 9 s, auto-reconnect 3500 ms, backoff ceiling 60 s after a 60 s grace period, ack resend 1500 ms, background disconnect 1 min. The 'Reconnecting' string is in site.xml. Chess.com says the board 'may not have reflected the current game state'. I did not check the commit date. | <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile/blob/main/lib/src/network/socket.dart> <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila/blob/master/translation/source/site.xml> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8593801-how-does-game-abandonment-work> |

I could not verify that figure, so it has low confidence and does not support any recommendation.
