**Product notes**

- **Apple HIG and Game Center / GameKit**: HIG Game Center (change log 2025-06-09): rules for the access point, achievements (four states), classic and recurring leaderboards, challenges (1 to 5 minutes), party codes, and join late, leave early, return. GameKit turn-based uses store and forward. The default turn timeout is one week. Invitations go out after the first turn. Players can send reminders and use exchanges. HIG onboarding (2024) and designing-for-games (2025): play at once, defer requests. App Review 4.5.4, 4.8, 5.1.1(v) and 1.2 apply only if King Down ships a native iOS app, but they are good practice anyway. <https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/game-center> <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/gamekit/creating-turn-based-games> <https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/>
- **Google Play Games Services**: Google ended its turn-based and real-time multiplayer APIs on 2020-03-31. PGS now covers only sign-in, achievements, leaderboards, friends and saves. The pages I read were updated 2026-10-06/08. Guidance includes guest play, prompting after a tutorial or rewarded moment, keeping local progress for later sync, the Recall API, automatic daily, weekly and all-time boards, and achievement counts and rules. <https://developer.android.com/games/pgs/quality> <https://developer.android.com/games/pgs/signin> <https://developer.android.com/games/pgs/achievements> <https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9469745>
- **Xbox Accessibility Guidelines and PlayFab**: XAG v3.2 (2023-06-08; index page updated 2026-08). XAG 106 covers screen narration, with a chess example and countdown narration every 7 to 10 s. XAG 116 covers time limits; real-time multiplayer is exempt from extension. XAG 119 covers chat speech-to-text and text-to-speech, preset previews and emoji names. PlayFab matchmaking examples (2026-04) show skill windows that widen with wait time and optional queue statistics. <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/accessibility/guidelines> <https://devdocs.xbox.com/build/game-principles/accessibility/xag-deep-dives/xag-116-time-limits> <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/playfab/multiplayer/matchmaking/config-examples>
- **W3C WCAG 2.2 and Game Accessibility Guidelines**: WCAG 2.2 criteria that matter most for a web chess game: 2.5.7 Dragging Movements, 2.5.8 Target Size (24 px), 2.2.1 Timing Adjustable (with the real-time exception), 4.1.3 Status Messages and 3.3.8 Accessible Authentication. The GAG full list (undated) supports a one-step start, symbol chat, no information by color alone, interactive tutorials, and no precise timing. <https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/dragging-movements.html> <https://gameaccessibilityguidelines.com/full-list/>
- **Nielsen Norman Group**: The relevant articles are older but still cited by current platform rules: login walls (2014), push notification mistakes (2018) and permission requests (2019, with the 12% and 81% figures). I could not open an NN/g article on social login; Baymard's sign-in article is from 2011 and is too old to use. <https://www.nngroup.com/articles/login-walls/> <https://www.nngroup.com/articles/push-notification/> <https://www.nngroup.com/articles/permission-requests/>
- **Lichess (lila and mobile source code)**: Open-source primary evidence: the disconnect timer formula, claim victory and call draw, the playban escalation, pool waves with block lists and behavior-aware pairing, chat presets, kid mode, per-event notification settings, the 80% correspondence alarm, the daily digest email, anonymous casual play, rating refunds for cheaters' victims, and the mobile socket reconnect settings. Code read on 2026-10-10. <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila> <https://github.com/lichess-org/mobile>
- **Chess.com**: Help center articles from 2024 to 2026: guest play matched by self-chosen level, the abandonment formula, first-move aborts, the sportsmanship policy, Daily chess with vacation, chat settings (everyone, request, friends, off), what a block does, achievements, and starting ratings of 400 or 800 by chosen level. Fair-play page (2026-08-25): about 85% of closures are automated and ongoing games go to the opponents. <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8593801-how-does-game-abandonment-work> <https://www.chess.com/fair-play>
- **Board Game Arena**: The FAQ is rendered by JavaScript and I could not read it. The studio docs show 'zombie mode': a leaver becomes a bot (level 0 to 3) so the others can finish, and the leaver loses karma. I could not confirm BGA turn-based time limits or its notification rules. <https://en.doc.boardgamearena.com/Zombie_Mode>
- **Words With Friends 2 (Zynga)**: Async word game. Games auto-resign after 5 days (random opponents) or 11 days (other opponents). There is a block list, 'Report Abuse', and a prompt to block after several declined game requests. I did not confirm nudge or chat defaults. <https://zyngasupport.helpshift.com/hc/en/63-words-with-friends-2/faq/10541-how-do-i-resign-from-a-game/> <https://zyngasupport.helpshift.com/hc/en/63-words-with-friends-2/faq/12368-what-happens-if-i-keep-declining-game-requests-from-another-player/>
- **Hearthstone**: Community wiki source, not official. Players talk to opponents only through six voluntary emotes, can squelch (mute) an opponent, and early builds rate-limited emotes. The wiki says Blizzard has no plans for text chat with opponents. <https://hearthstone.wiki.gg/wiki/Emote>
- **League of Legends**: Community wiki source, not official. A remake vote opens after an early disconnect: the leaver takes a loss and a 'Leaver' flag (LeaverBuster), and the other players get no loss. The page gives no dates for the remake rules. <https://wiki.leagueoflegends.com/en-us/Remake>
- **OpenAI Apps SDK (ChatGPT plugins)**: Per-tool noauth or oauth2 sign-in with a linking UI. Display modes: inline (at most 2 actions, no nested scroll), fullscreen, and PiP for games (may show as fullscreen on mobile). No documented push or background updates. Audience must suit ages 13 to 17. No digital goods sales. destructiveHint for irreversible actions. The pages are undated, and the platform changes often, so recheck before building. <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/build/auth> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/concepts/ui-guidelines> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/app-submission-guidelines> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/reference>
- **Web push (Chromium, WebKit, Android)**: Chrome 80+ shows a quieter UI for sites with low opt-in rates. Notifications are 74% of permission prompts, with a 10% grant rate on desktop and 21% on Android (USENIX 2021). iOS 16.4+ web push needs a Home Screen web app and a user gesture. Android 13+ has notifications off by default and advises asking in context. <https://blog.chromium.org/2020/01/introducing-quieter-permission-ui-for.html> <https://webkit.org/blog/13878/web-push-for-web-apps-on-ios-and-ipados/> <https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/notification-permission>
- **Child safety (ICO Children's Code, Thriving in Games Group)**: High privacy by default and no nudges toward lower privacy (ICO). Digital Thriving Playbook (2026-05): limit stranger contact for child accounts, plan for report abuse, and treat player trust as the main safety metric. The Fair Play Alliance site now redirects to the Thriving in Games Group, and I did not find its older Disruption and Harms framework there. <https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/childrens-information/childrens-code-guidance-and-resources/age-appropriate-design-a-code-of-practice-for-online-services/7-default-settings/> <https://digitalthrivingplaybook.org/guide/safety-by-design-as-a-gameplay-practice/>
- **Game UX literature (Celia Hodent, GDC)**: Usability heuristics plus engage-ability. Onboarding rules: learn by doing, at most three new items at once, no punishment during onboarding, and 'show the locks before giving the keys'. I found no primary GDC source with a 'time to first match' benchmark number. <https://celiahodent.com/understanding-the-success-of-fortnite-ux/> <https://celiahodent.com/gamers-brain-ux-onboarding/>

**Open questions**

- Does OpenAI's current Apps/Plugins platform offer any app-initiated message, notification or background widget update? I found none in the reference, but absence of documentation is not proof. This decides how async turns reach ChatGPT players.
- What is the youngest expected player on kingdown.dev? If under-13 players are in scope, chat defaults, data collection (COPPA, ICO Children's Code) and the profile visibility of display names and pictures need a stricter design.
- Does the owner want rated async games at launch? Engine help is easy in async play because the computer opponent runs in the browser, so fair-play enforcement must exist before rated async play opens.
- How should clocks and disconnect timers count King Down turns when Haste gives one side two actions in a row? This affects the abort rule, 'first move' limits and the screen-reader announcements.
- What time controls and per-move deadlines suit King Down's longer, more tactical games? Lichess and Chess.com numbers come from standard chess and may not fit.
- With tens to a few hundred players online, should the lobby show the number of players and open seeks (social proof) or hide them (PlayFab notes both options)? A test with real players can decide this.
- The shared web-search budget ran out partway through. I could not verify claims about Clash Royale, Marvel Snap, Roblox, Fortnite, the Board Game Arena FAQ, Riot's LeaverBuster pages or the NN/g social-login article. A follow-up run can fill these gaps if needed.
- The USENIX 2021 grant rates appear as 10%/21% in Google's abstract and as 12%/23% in a secondary summary. The exact figures do not change any recommendation.

## Account and identity journey for online games (web and embedded) [`ux-accounts`]

Guest play first, then an account, is the normal journey. Lichess and Chess.com both let people play with no account. An account unlocks ratings, game history and social features. Game back ends (Unity, Supercell ID, Discord provisional accounts, Supabase anonymous sign-in) use the same pattern: an anonymous player first, then a link to a real sign-in. ChatGPT supports this directly. A tool can declare both "noauth" and "oauth2", so play starts anonymously and linking unlocks more. King Down already has one identity store for both surfaces: the Supabase OAuth 2.1 server issues tokens for the same project users. So one account and crossplay need no new identity system. What they need is careful UX: a unique public handle chosen at first sign-in, no real names or face photos by default, a visible "signed in as @handle" on the ChatGPT consent page, and a way to link more sign-in methods. Chess sites do not ask for a birth date. They state a minimum age, keep social features off or limited, and act when they learn a user is a child. Regulators push "high privacy by default" (ICO code, FTC v. Epic). Deletion must start in the app, with a web link, and must remove personal data. Chess sites keep games but make the deleted player anonymous. King Down's match tables delete the opponent's game when one player deletes their account. That is a gap. Passkeys are growing, but no chess site I checked offers them, and Supabase passkeys are still experimental. Note: the shared web-search budget ran out during this task. Some products (Discord help, Epic, Roblox, Steam, Board Game Arena, Xbox) could not be read in full.

To upgrade: updateUser({email}) then verify the email, or linkIdentity({provider}) (manual linking must be on; manual linking is beta). Policies must read the is_anonymous claim from the JWT. Supabase strongly recommends invisible CAPTCHA or Cloudflare Turnstile. The default limit is 30 anonymous sign-ins per hour per IP. Automatic cleanup does not exist; the docs give SQL that deletes anonymous users older than 30 days. If the email already belongs to another user, the upgrade fails. The app must then move the guest's rows and resolve conflicts (overwrite or merge). Anonymous users cannot register passkeys. Supabase PKCE page: 'the code exchange must be initiated on the same browser and device where the flow was started.' **Product notes**

- **Lichess**: Anonymous casual play; account for rated play, tournaments and analysis. Sign-in: username or email + password, 'Log in by email' link, TOTP 2FA. No social sign-in, no passkeys, no avatars (country flag and flair only). Username 2-20 characters, cannot change (one case change). Close / close forever / delete; usernames never reused. One-way follow, where mutual follow = friend. Privacy: challenge default 'if registered', messages default 'always', Kid mode turns off all communication. Up to 200 ongoing games per account (100 real-time). OAuth PKCE 'login with Lichess' for third-party apps. Facts come from source code and translation files (master branch, read 2026-10-10) and the privacy policy (2022-03-16). <https://github.com/lichess-org/lila> <https://lichess.org/privacy> <https://lichess.org/faq>
- **Chess.com**: Guest Play by skill level (2026-02). Sign-in: Apple, Google, Facebook, username/password. Username: 3 or more characters, change every 90 days, no impersonation; real names discouraged. Activation email before chat, posts or avatar change. No birth date asked; per-country minimum ages (13-18); minors muted until a parent consents; Safe Mode. Deletion: 10-day grace, games and posts anonymized, in-app path on iOS/Android. Two-way friend requests; challenges from Everyone/Friends/Nobody; chat controls; block. Help articles are dated 2026. <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/8615312-what-is-guest-play-i-can-play-without-an-account> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/9829268-how-do-i-delete-my-chess-com-account> <https://support.chess.com/en/articles/11070988-why-do-i-need-parental-consent>
- **ChessKid**: Separate product for children ('ages 13 and under', 2026-03-09). Kid and adult accounts; guardians are linked by email. Custom usernames are reviewed by staff (up to 24 hours). Adults cannot talk to kids; kids cannot chat with each other; friends are only for seeing who is online and challenging them; guardians can turn off social access. I could not confirm a kidSAFE certificate or generated usernames. <https://www.chesskid.com/article/view/how-to-understand-chesskids-safety-features2> <https://support.chesskid.com/en/articles/8863001-what-age-range-is-chesskid-designed-for> <https://www.chesskid.com/learn/articles/getting-started-with-chesskid>
- **OGS (online-go.com)**: Open-source code (read 2026-10-10). Sign-in with Google, Facebook, Twitter, Apple, GitHub, or username/password with optional email. The form warns that the username is public, so do not use your email. I could not check guest play or privacy settings (the site needs JavaScript). <https://github.com/online-go/online-go.com>
- **Discord**: 2023 move from name#1234 to a unique lowercase username plus a free display name, because friend requests failed. Activities (iframe apps) sign users in with an authorize/authenticate flow. The Social SDK (2025-03) offers provisional accounts that merge into Discord accounts later; key your data on your own ID. The help center blocked fetches (HTTP 403), so I could not check privacy settings or age-assurance details. <https://discord.com/blog/usernames> <https://docs.discord.com/developers/activities/building-an-activity> <https://docs.discord.com/developers/discord-social-sdk/development-guides/using-provisional-accounts>
- **PlayStation Network**: Online ID: first change free, later changes paid; old ID can show for 30 days; child accounts cannot change IDs; some older games break after a change. <https://www.playstation.com/en-us/support/account/change-psn-online-id/>
- **Epic Games / Fortnite**: Read only through the FTC record (Epic pages returned 403). In 2022 Epic paid a $275M COPPA penalty. Voice and text chat were on by default. The order requires chat off by default for children and teens, and a neutral age gate. <https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2022/12/fortnite-video-game-maker-epic-games-pay-more-half-billion-dollars-over-ftc-allegations>
- **Supercell ID**: One free ID per player saves progress across devices and across Supercell games, carries friends between games, and lets players switch accounts on one device. <https://supercell.com/en/supercell-id/>
- **Unity Authentication**: Anonymous sign-in, with a cached session token per install. An unlinked account is lost on reinstall. Unity recommends linking at least one external identity provider. <https://docs.unity.com/ugs/en-us/manual/authentication/manual/use-anon-sign-in>
- **Supabase Auth**: Anonymous sign-in (CAPTCHA recommended, no automatic cleanup), automatic same-email linking, manual linkIdentity (beta), unlimited sessions by default, optional single session (Pro). OAuth 2.1 server with DCR for MCP clients. Email link or 6-digit code, 1-hour expiry. Passkeys are experimental (2026-05-28) and need a confirmed, non-anonymous user and one RP ID. <https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/auth-anonymous> <https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/oauth-server> <https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/passkeys> <https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/sessions>
- **ChatGPT (OpenAI Apps SDK / plugins)**: Per-tool noauth/oauth2 schemes; a linking prompt appears on an mcp/www_authenticate error. CIMD or DCR. Anonymized openai/subject and openai/session in tool _meta. More than one account through an openai/profile tool. Rules: audience 13-17 allowed, no targeting of under-13s, minimal data, demo account for review. The OpenAI help center returned 403, so I could not check how users unlink, or teen and parental-control effects on plugins. <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/build/auth> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/reference> <https://developers.openai.com/apps-sdk/app-submission-guidelines>
- **Apple App Store / Google Play**: Only for a future native app. Apple 4.8 (privacy-focused login when social login is offered; changed 2024-01). Apple 5.1.1(v): use without login where possible, in-app deletion. Google Play: in-app deletion plus a web deletion link (enforced after 2024-05-31). <https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/> <https://developer.apple.com/support/offering-account-deletion-in-your-app/> <https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13327111>
- **Board Game Arena, Steam, Xbox, Roblox**: Not studied in depth. The BGA site needs JavaScript (no readable FAQ). The Xbox support page returned no text. I did not reach Steam or Roblox before the shared web-search budget ran out. Do not use these as evidence. <https://en.boardgamearena.com/faq> <https://support.xbox.com/en-US/help/account-profile/profile/change-gamertag>

**Open questions**

- Does ChatGPT on iOS and Android open the OAuth consent page in a system browser? Google sign-in fails in embedded web views. The OpenAI help center returned HTTP 403, so I could not check.
- Is openai/subject stable across conversations, devices and app reinstalls? OpenAI has not answered the community question (Dec 2025 / Jan 2026).
- Do ChatGPT teen accounts or parental controls limit plugins? OpenAI pages returned 403.
- Will King Down ship a native iOS or Android app? Apple 4.8, Apple in-app deletion and Google Play web-deletion rules apply only then.
- Which countries does King Down target, and where is the owner legally based? Chess.com lists 18 as the minimum age for Israel and 16 for Germany, Spain and the Netherlands. A lawyer should set the minimum age in the terms.
- Will King Down add free-text chat or private messages? That decision drives most of the moderation and child-safety work.
- Do Supabase anonymous users count toward monthly active user billing? I did not verify this.
- Should a guest be able to accept a challenge link from a signed-in friend (Lichess allows anonymous casual games)? Or must both players sign in for server matches?
- Research limit: the shared web-search budget (200 calls) ran out during this task. Discord help, Epic, Roblox, Steam, Xbox and Board Game Arena pages were blocked or not reached. Those platforms rest only on primary pages I could fetch directly, or they are left out.
